Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the chart rendering system. Let me create a detailed summary:

## Chart Rendering System - Comprehensive Exploration

I've thoroughly explored the chart rendering system in this codebase. Here's what I found:

---

### 1. **How Chart Specs Are Generated (Backend - `agent.py`)**

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.worktrees/chart-library-switch/backend/app/agent.py`

**Key Points:**
- **Lines 27, 51, 56-62:** System prompt instructs the orchestrator agent to call `render_chart` tool directly with Plotly specs
- **Lines 326-329:** Backend captures `render_chart` tool inputs in `tool_chart_specs` dict, keyed by tool ID
- **Lines 485-492:** When processing assistant messages containing tool_use blocks, the backend checks if it's a `render_chart` call and stores the input (which contains `data` and `layout`)
- **Lines 624-625:** If a tool returns success and a chart_spec was captured, it's attached to the tool_result SSE event data
- **Lines 649-656:** Chart specs are accumulated in `assistant_metadata["chart_specs"]` for persistence

**Chart Spec Structure (from mcp_sse.py lines 252-257):**
```python
{
    "status": "success",
    "chart_spec": {
        "data": [...],      # Array of Plotly trace objects
        "layout": {...}     # Plotly layout object with required 'title'
    }
}
```

---

### 2. **Backend-to-Frontend Data Flow (SSE Events)**

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.worktrees/chart-library-switch/backend/app/mcp_sse.py`

**MCP Tool Definition (lines 84-109):**
- Tool name: `render_chart`
- Inputs: `data` (array of Plotly traces) and `layout` (object with required `title`)
- Returns: `{"status": "success", "chart_spec": {data, layout}}`

**SSE Event Flow:**
```
Backend stream_chat() sends JSON SSE events:
  event: tool_call
  data: {"id": "...", "name": "render_chart", "input": {...}}
  
  event: tool_result
  data: {"id": "...", "name": "...", "chart_spec": {data, layout}}
```

**Key Structure in agent.py:**
- Lines 243, 329: `tool_chart_specs` dict stores chart spec inputs from tool_use blocks
- Line 665: `result_data["chart_spec"]` is set from `tool_chart_specs[tool_id]` when available
- Line 656: Chart specs appended to `assistant_metadata["chart_specs"]` for persistence

---

### 3. **Frontend ChartWidget Component**

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.worktrees/chart-library-switch/frontend/src/components/ChartWidget.tsx`

**Component Details:**
```tsx
export function ChartWidget({ data, layout, frames }: ChartWidgetProps) {
  // Input validation: checks if any trace has non-empty data fields
  const DATA_FIELDS = ['x', 'y', 'z', 'values', 'labels', 'lat', 'lon', 'r', 'theta',
    'lowerfence', 'q1', 'median', 'q3', 'upperfence'];
  
  // Renders react-plotly.js Plot component with:
  // - config: { responsive: true, displayModeBar: true }
  // - Automatic theme switching (dark/light mode support)
  // - Auto-sizing layout
  // - Plotly v3 compatible title normalization
}
```

**Key Features:**
- **Line 65:** `displayModeBar: true` — enables the Plotly toolbar (includes export button)
- **Lines 33-36:** Title normalization for Plotly v3 ({text: "..."} format)
- **Lines 43-56:** Theme-aware styling with dark mode colors
- **Lines 40-41:** Handles frames (for animated charts)

---

### 4. **Export Button Location**

**Where it is:** Inside the Plotly interactive mode bar (top-right of chart)

**How it works:**
- **Built-in Plotly feature:** The `displayModeBar: true` config (line 65 of ChartWidget) enables Plotly's native toolbar
- **Export buttons provided by Plotly:**
  - Download as PNG
  - Download as SVG
  - Download as JPEG
  - Camera icon for snapshot

**No custom export button exists in the codebase** — the application relies entirely on Plotly's built-in export functionality.

---

### 5. **Chart Configuration & Settings**

**Frontend Configuration (ChartWidget.tsx lines 59-66):**
```tsx
<Plot
  data={data as Plotly.Data[]}
  layout={{
    autosize: true,           // Responsive to container
    height: 400,              // Fixed height
    ...themeLayout,           // Dark/light theme colors
    ...normalizedLayout       // Agent-provided layout
  } as Partial<Plotly.Layout>}
  frames={resolvedFrames as Plotly.Frame[] | undefined}
  useResizeHandler            // Resize on container change
  style={{ width: '100%' }}
  config={{
    responsive: true,         // Mobile-responsive
    displayModeBar: true      // Show toolbar with export options
  }}
/>
```

**Backend Validation (mcp_sse.py lines 230-251):**
- Validates that at least one trace has non-empty data fields
- Rejects specs with all-empty traces
- Minimal validation — Plotly is forgiving for trace-specific properties

**Supported Chart Types:**
Based on system prompt (agent.py lines 66-69):
- bar, line, scatter, pie, histogram, box, heatmap, 3d scatter, surface, contour, waterfall, funnel, sunburst, treemap, and more

---

### 6. **Data Types & Interfaces**

**TypeScript Types (types.ts):**

```typescript
export interface ToolCallResult {
  chart_spec?: {
    data: unknown[];
    layout?: Record<string, unknown>;
    frames?: unknown[];
  };
}

export interface ContentSegment {
  type: 'tool' | 'subagent_end' | ... ;
  chart_spec?: {
    data: unknown[];
    layout?: Record<string, unknown>;
    frames?: unknown[];
  };
}
```

---

### 7. **Rendering Flow (Complete Chain)**

```
Backend (agent.py):
  1. Agent calls render_chart({data, layout})
  2. Backend captures input in tool_chart_specs dict
  3. MCP tool returns {status: success, chart_spec}
  4. SSE event emitted: tool_result with chart_spec

Frontend (agentService.ts):
  1. SSE event parsed in handleSSEEvent()
  2. 'tool_result' case extracts chart_spec (line 218)
  3. ToolCallResult created with chart_spec
  4. onToolResult() callback invoked

Frontend (MessageBubble.tsx):
  1. Segments created from tool results
  2. Chart segments detected (line 367: s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult?.chart_spec)
  3. Rendered via InlineQueryResult or ChartWidget (lines 371-374)

InlineQueryResult.tsx (lines 87-101):
  if (result.chart_spec) {
    return <ChartWidget data={...} layout={...} frames={...} />
  }
```

---

### 8. **Key Dependencies**

**Frontend (package.json lines 13-18):**
```json
"plotly.js-dist-min": "^3.4.0",
"react-plotly.js": "^2.6.0",
"@types/react-plotly.js": "^2.6.4",
"@types/plotly.js-dist-min": "^2.3.4"
```

**Backend:**
- Claude Agent SDK (`AgentDefinition` for subagent orchestration)
- FastAPI / Starlette for MCP SSE transport

---

### 9. **Important Design Decisions**

1. **No server-side rendering:** Charts are rendered client-side via Plotly.js (not PNG/image generation)
2. **No custom export UI:** Relies on Plotly's built-in toolbar export buttons
3. **Agent-built specs:** The LLM constructs the full Plotly JSON spec, backend is pass-through validation only
4. **Structured tool output:** `render_chart` is an MCP tool (not free-text) to ensure required `layout.title`
5. **Streaming-friendly:** Charts render inline as they complete, interleaved with text narrative
6. **Theme support:** Charts auto-adapt to dark/light mode via theme-aware CSS colors

---

### 10. **File Summary**

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `backend/app/agent.py` | System prompt, tool capture logic, SSE event emission |
| `backend/app/mcp_sse.py` | `render_chart` MCP tool definition & validation |
| `frontend/src/components/ChartWidget.tsx` | React component wrapping Plotly |
| `frontend/src/components/InlineQueryResult.tsx` | Routes chart_spec to ChartWidget or table rendering |
| `frontend/src/components/MessageBubble.tsx` | Segments and displays charts in answer blocks |
| `frontend/src/agent/agentService.ts` | Parses SSE events and extracts chart_spec |
| `frontend/src/types.ts` | TypeScript interfaces for chart data structures |

---

This completes the thorough exploration of the chart rendering system. The system is clean, modular, and relies heavily on Plotly's built-in capabilities for interactive visualization and export functionality.

     1→import { useEffect, useRef, useCallback } from 'react';

     7→import type { TableInfo } from '../types';
     8→import './AgentPanel.css';
     9→import { exportConversation } from '../utils/exportConversation';

    11→
    12→interface AgentPanelProps {
    13→  tables: TableInfo[];
    14→  onUpload: (files: File[]) => Promise<void>;
    15→  onLoadSample: () => Promise<void>;
    16→  pendingSkillCommand?: string | null;
    17→  onSkillCommandConsumed?: () => void;

    20→export function AgentPanel({ tables, onUpload, onLoadSample, pendingSkillCommand, onSkillCommandConsumed }: AgentPanelProps) {
    21→  const { t } = useTranslation();
    22→  const { messages, clearMessages } = useAgent();
    23→  const { activeConversationId, startNewConversation } = useConversation();
    24→  const bottomRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
    25→  const scrollContainerRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
    26→  const userScrolledUp = useRef(false);
    27→
    28→  const isNearBottom = useCallback(() => {
    29→    const el = scrollContainerRef.current;
    30→    if (!el) return true;
    31→    return el.scrollHeight - el.scrollTop - el.clientHeight < 80;
    32→  }, []);
    33→
    34→  const handleScroll = useCallback(() => {
    35→    userScrolledUp.current = !isNearBottom();
    36→  }, [isNearBottom]);
    37→
    38→  useEffect(() => {
    39→    if (!userScrolledUp.current) {
    40→      bottomRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth' });
    41→    }
    42→  }, [messages]);
    43→
    44→  return (
    45→    <div className="agent-panel">
    46→      <div className="agent-panel__header">
    47→        <span className="agent-panel__title">{t('agentMode')}</span>
    48→        <div className="agent-panel__actions">
    49→          {messages.length > 0 && (
    50→            <button className="agent-panel__clear" onClick={() => exportConversation()}>
    51→              {t('export')}
    52→            </button>
    53→          )}
    54→          {messages.length > 0 && (
    55→            <button className="agent-panel__clear" onClick={() => { if (confirm(t('clearConfirm'))) { clearMessages(activeConversationId); startNewConversation(); } }}>
    56→              {t('clear')}
    57→            </button>
    58→          )}
    59→        </div>
    60→      </div>
    61→      <div className="agent-panel__messages" ref={scrollContainerRef} onScroll={handleScroll}>
    62→        {messages.length === 0 && (
    63→          <div className="agent-panel__empty">
    64→            {tables.length === 0 ? (
    65→              <FileUpload onUpload={onUpload} onLoadSample={onLoadSample} />
    66→            ) : (
    67→              t('agentEmptyState')
    68→            )}
    69→          </div>
    70→        )}
    71→        {messages.map((msg, index) => (
    72→          <MessageBubble key={msg.id} message={msg} messageIndex={index} />
    73→        ))}
    74→        <div ref={bottomRef} />
    75→      </div>
    76→      <ChatInput pendingSkillCommand={pendingSkillCommand} onSkillCommandConsumed={onSkillCommandConsumed} />
    77→    </div>
    78→  );
    79→}

Now let me propose approaches. Here are **3 approaches** with trade-offs:

---

### Approach A: Dual render_chart tools (Recommended)

- Define two MCP tools: `render_chart_plotly` and `render_chart_vegalite`
- Backend dynamically registers only the tool matching the user's current library preference
- Frontend sends `chart_library` preference with each chat request
- Agent system prompt adjusted per-library to instruct correct spec format
- Frontend has `ChartWidget` (Plotly) and a new `VegaLiteChartWidget` side by side, selected by a `library` field in `chart_spec`

**Pros:** Clean separation, no ambiguity for the agent, each tool has its own schema validation
**Cons:** Two tool definitions to maintain, slight duplication

---

### Approach B: Single render_chart tool with library parameter

- Keep one `render_chart` tool, add a `library` field (`"plotly"` or `"vegalite"`)
- Backend passes the user's preference to the agent via system prompt
- Agent emits the appropriate spec format and sets the `library` field
- Frontend dispatches to Plotly or Vega-Lite renderer based on `library` field

**Pros:** Single tool, simpler tool registry
**Cons:** One schema must accommodate both formats (harder to validate), agent could produce wrong format

---

### Approach C: Frontend conversion (rejected per your answer)

Already ruled out — you want native Vega-Lite specs from the agent.

---

**My recommendation: Approach B** (single tool with library field). Reasons:
- Simpler to maintain one tool definition
- The agent is already good at following system prompt instructions for spec format
- The `library` field in `chart_spec` makes frontend routing trivial
- Schema validation can be light (both libraries accept `data`/`layout` or `$schema`/`mark`/`encoding`)

Do you prefer Approach A (dual tools) or Approach B (single tool with library param)?

     1→import Plot from 'react-plotly.js';
     2→import { useTheme } from '../hooks/useTheme';
     3→import { useTranslation } from '../hooks/useTranslation';
     4→
     5→interface ChartWidgetProps {
     6→  data: unknown[];
     7→  layout?: Record<string, unknown>;
     8→  frames?: unknown[];
     9→}
    10→
    11→export function ChartWidget({ data, layout, frames }: ChartWidgetProps) {
    12→  const { theme } = useTheme();
    13→  const isDark = theme === 'dark';
    14→  const { t } = useTranslation();
    15→
    16→  // Guard: don't render a blank Plotly chart when all traces have empty data
    17→  const DATA_FIELDS = ['x', 'y', 'z', 'values', 'labels', 'lat', 'lon', 'r', 'theta',
    18→    'lowerfence', 'q1', 'median', 'q3', 'upperfence'];
    19→  const hasData = Array.isArray(data) && data.some((trace) => {
    20→    if (!trace || typeof trace !== 'object') return false;
    21→    const t = trace as Record<string, unknown>;
    22→    return DATA_FIELDS.some((f) => Array.isArray(t[f]) && (t[f] as unknown[]).length > 0);
    23→  });
    24→  if (!hasData) {
    25→    return (
    26→      <div style={{ padding: '1rem', textAlign: 'center', opacity: 0.6 }}>
    27→        {t('chartNoData')}
    28→      </div>
    29→    );
    30→  }
    31→
    32→  // Normalize layout.title: Plotly v3 requires {text: "..."} object form
    33→  const normalizedLayout = { ...layout };
    34→  if (typeof normalizedLayout.title === 'string') {
    35→    normalizedLayout.title = { text: normalizedLayout.title };
    36→  }
    37→
    38→  // frames can live at the top level or inside layout (common variant);
    39→  // extract from layout so Plotly receives them as a dedicated prop.
    40→  const resolvedFrames = frames || (normalizedLayout.frames as unknown[]);
    41→  delete normalizedLayout.frames;
    42→
    43→  // Theme-aware colors for Plotly
    44→  const themeLayout = isDark
    45→    ? {
    46→        paper_bgcolor: 'rgba(0,0,0,0)',
    47→        plot_bgcolor: 'rgba(0,0,0,0)',
    48→        font: { color: '#e2e8f0' },
    49→        xaxis: { gridcolor: '#374151', zerolinecolor: '#374151' },
    50→        yaxis: { gridcolor: '#374151', zerolinecolor: '#374151' },
    51→        legend: { font: { color: '#e2e8f0' } },
    52→      }
    53→    : {
    54→        paper_bgcolor: 'rgba(0,0,0,0)',
    55→        plot_bgcolor: 'rgba(0,0,0,0)',
    56→      };
    57→
    58→  return (
    59→    <Plot
    60→      data={data as Plotly.Data[]}
    61→      layout={{ autosize: true, height: 400, ...themeLayout, ...normalizedLayout } as Partial<Plotly.Layout>}
    62→      frames={resolvedFrames as Plotly.Frame[] | undefined}
    63→      useResizeHandler
    64→      style={{ width: '100%' }}
    65→      config={{ responsive: true, displayModeBar: true }}
    66→    />
    67→  );
    68→}
    69→
```

     1→import asyncio
     2→import json
     3→import logging
     4→from typing import AsyncIterator
     5→
     6→from claude_agent_sdk import AgentDefinition
     7→from app.database import Database
     8→from app.config import (
     9→    ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL_SDK,
    10→    SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK,
    11→    BACKEND_BASE_URL,
    12→    LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY, LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY, LANGFUSE_BASE_URL, LANGFUSE_ENABLED,
    13→    SDK_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS,
    14→)
    15→from app.memory_store import memory_store
    16→from app.agent_memory import read_memories
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→
    21→def build_system_prompt(db: Database) -> str:
    22→    tables = db.list_tables()
    23→    prompt = """You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.
    24→
    25→Tools at your disposal:
    26→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__execute_sql — run SQL queries against DuckDB
    27→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__render_chart — render a Plotly chart
    28→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__ask_user_question — ask the user a clarifying question
    29→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__create_skill — create a reusable skill (workflow template) that can be invoked later via /skill-name
    30→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__save_memory — save a fact, preference, or pattern to long-term memory
    31→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__recall_memories — retrieve stored memories
    32→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__forget_memory — remove a specific memory
    33→- Task tool with subagent_type "sql-analyst" — delegate complex multi-query data exploration
    34→
    35→Skill creation workflow (follow this exactly):
    36→When the user asks you to create a skill, do NOT call create_skill immediately. Follow this process:
    37→1. If the user's request is vague or unclear about what the skill should do, use ask_user_question to clarify the skill's purpose, scope, and key steps BEFORE drafting any plan. Keep asking until you have enough detail.
    38→2. Once the requirements are clear, draft a plan for the skill: propose a name, description, and outline of the content (step-by-step instructions).
    39→3. Present the plan to the user using ask_user_question with options like "Looks good, create it" and "I want to make changes". Describe the full plan in the question text.
    40→4. If the user wants changes, update the plan based on their feedback and present the revised plan again (repeat step 3).
    41→5. Only after the user explicitly approves the plan, call create_skill with the finalized name, description, and content.
    42→- NEVER skip the review step. The user must approve before the skill is created.
    43→
    44→Task tool usage:
    45→- When using the Task tool, set "subagent_type" to "sql-analyst". NEVER use generic values like "general-purpose" or any other value.
    46→- Do NOT set the "model" parameter on the Task tool — it is already configured.
    47→- After the sql-analyst returns, do NOT repeat the data tables, numbers, or SQL queries. Simply add brief commentary on what the data means. Never include SQL in your answer unless the user explicitly asks to see the SQL.
    48→
    49→Direct tool usage:
    50→- For simple SQL queries, call execute_sql directly instead of delegating to sql-analyst.
    51→- For charts/visualizations, call execute_sql to get the data, then call render_chart yourself with the Plotly spec. Do NOT delegate charting to a subagent.
    52→
    53→Charting workflow (follow this exactly):
    54→1. Run execute_sql to get the data you need for a chart.
    55→2. Call render_chart with TWO required parameters:
    56→   - `data`: array of Plotly trace objects (e.g. [{"type": "bar", "x": [...], "y": [...]}])
    57→   - `layout`: object that MUST include `title` (e.g. {"title": "My Chart"})
    58→   Both `data` and `layout` are required — the tool WILL accept both. Do not second-guess this.
    59→3. After the chart renders, write your narrative text discussing what the chart shows.
    60→4. Repeat steps 1-3 for each additional chart. This produces interleaved charts and narrative.
    61→- Do NOT render all charts first and then write all narrative at the end.
    62→- Do NOT output chart JSON as a code block. Always use the render_chart tool.
    63→- NEVER include Plotly JSON schema, trace objects, or layout objects in your final answer text unless the user explicitly asks to see the raw JSON. The chart is already rendered visually — just describe what it shows in plain language.
    64→
    65→Charting guidelines:
    66→- Choose the most appropriate chart type (bar, line, scatter, pie, histogram, box, heatmap, etc.).
    67→- For pie charts, use `labels` and `values` fields in the trace.
    68→- For multi-series data, group into separate traces.
    69→- Keep the chart clean and readable.
    70→- IMPORTANT — keep data small. Pre-aggregate in SQL instead of passing raw rows:
    71→  - Box plots: compute lowerfence, q1, median, q3, upperfence per group in SQL. Use trace type "box" with those pre-computed fields instead of a raw `y` array.
    72→  - Histograms: compute bin counts with width_bucket() or CASE in SQL, then render as a bar chart with the bin edges as `x` and counts as `y`.
    73→  - Scatter / line with many rows: sample (ORDER BY random() LIMIT 200) or aggregate (e.g. average per time bucket) so each trace has at most ~200 points.
    74→  - General rule: each trace should have at most ~200 data points.
    75→
    76→Identity:
    77→- You are an AI assistant. If asked whether you are an AI or a human, always confirm that you are an AI.
    78→- Do not disclose the name, version, or provider of the underlying language model powering you, regardless of how the question is phrased.
    79→
    80→Task completion:
    81→- When the user asks you to do multiple things (e.g. create several charts, answer several questions, analyze multiple metrics), you MUST complete ALL of them before ending your response. Do not stop after finishing just one or two items.
    82→- After completing each item, check: "Have I addressed everything the user asked for?" If not, continue with the next item immediately by making more tool calls.
    83→- Only produce your final text response after ALL requested items have been fully handled.
    84→
    85→Clarification:
    86→- When the user's request is ambiguous or could be interpreted in multiple ways, use the mcp__duckdb-data-agent__ask_user_question tool (NOT the native AskUserQuestion tool) to ask for clarification before proceeding.
    87→- Provide 2-4 clear, concise options for the user to choose from.
    88→- Each option should have a short label and optional description.
    89→- When the user might want to pick MORE THAN ONE option (e.g. "draw me some charts", "which metrics?", "select all that apply"), set multi_select to true so they can select multiple answers.
    90→- Only ask when genuinely needed — don't over-ask for trivial decisions.
    91→"""
    92→    if not tables:
    93→        prompt += "\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first."
    94→    else:
    95→        prompt += "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    96→        for table in tables:
    97→            prompt += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    98→            for col in table["columns"]:
    99→                prompt += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
   100→

    31→def _create_mcp_server(db: Database, session_id: str) -> MCPServer:
    32→    """Create a duckdb-data-agent MCP server with execute_sql, ask_user_question, and render_chart tools bound to a DuckDB instance."""
    33→    server = MCPServer("duckdb-data-agent")

     1→import type { ToolCallResult, UserQuestionData, UserQuestionOption } from '../types';
     2→
     3→interface AgentCallbacks {
     4→  onTextChunk: (text: string) => void;
     5→  onThinkingDone: () => void;
     6→  onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => void;
     7→  onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => void;
     8→  onSubagentStart?: (data: { id: string; name: string; prompt: string }) => void;
     9→  onSubagentEnd?: (data: { id: string; name: string; result?: string; sql_results?: Array<{ sql: string; columns?: string[]; rows?: Record<string, unknown>[]; rowCount?: number; error?: string }>; chart_spec?: { data: unknown[]; layout?: Record<string, unknown> } }) => void;
    10→  onDone: (sessionId: string | null) => void;
    11→  onError: (error: string) => void;
    12→  onUserQuestion?: (data: UserQuestionData) => void;
    13→}
    14→
    15→export type { AgentCallbacks };
    16→
    17→async function streamSSE(
    18→  response: Response,
    19→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
    20→  signal?: AbortSignal,
    21→): Promise<void> {
    22→  const reader = response.body?.getReader();
    23→  if (!reader) {
    24→    callbacks.onError('No response stream');
    25→    return;
    26→  }
    27→
    28→  let doneReceived = false;
    29→  const wrappedCallbacks: AgentCallbacks = {
    30→    ...callbacks,
    31→    onDone: (sessionId) => {
    32→      doneReceived = true;
    33→      callbacks.onDone(sessionId);
    34→    },
    35→    onError: (error) => {
    36→      doneReceived = true;
    37→      callbacks.onError(error);
    38→    },
    39→  };
    40→
    41→  try {
    42→    const decoder = new TextDecoder();
    43→    let buffer = '';
    44→    let eventType = '';
    45→
    46→    while (true) {
    47→      const { done, value } = await reader.read();
    48→      if (done) break;
    49→
    50→      buffer += decoder.decode(value, { stream: true });
    51→      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
    52→      buffer = lines.pop() ?? '';
    53→      for (const line of lines) {
    54→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    55→          eventType = line.slice(7).trim();
    56→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    57→          const data = line.slice(6);
    58→          try {
    59→            const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
    60→            handleSSEEvent(eventType, parsed, wrappedCallbacks);
    61→          } catch {
    62→            // Skip malformed JSON
    63→          }
    64→          eventType = '';
    65→        }
    66→      }
    67→    }
    68→
    69→    // Process any remaining data left in buffer after stream ends
    70→    if (buffer.trim()) {
    71→      const remainingLines = buffer.split('\n');
    72→      for (const line of remainingLines) {
    73→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    74→          eventType = line.slice(7).trim();
    75→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    76→          const data = line.slice(6);
    77→          try {
    78→            const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
    79→            handleSSEEvent(eventType, parsed, wrappedCallbacks);
    80→          } catch {
    81→            // Skip malformed JSON
    82→          }
    83→          eventType = '';
    84→        }
    85→      }
    86→    }
    87→
    88→    // Safety net: if stream ended without done/error, force done
    89→    if (!doneReceived) {
    90→      callbacks.onDone(null);
    91→    }
    92→  } catch (e: unknown) {
    93→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
    94→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
    95→    callbacks.onError(msg);
    96→  }
    97→}
    98→
    99→export async function runAgentLoop(
   100→  message: string,
   101→  agentSessionId: string | null,
   102→  langfuseSessionId: string | null,
   103→  conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[] | null,
   104→  conversationId?: string | null,
   105→  callbacks?: AgentCallbacks,
   106→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   107→  userSessionId?: string,
   108→  skills?: string[],
   109→): Promise<void> {
   110→  if (!callbacks) return;
   111→  try {
   112→    const response = await fetch('/api/chat', {
   113→      method: 'POST',
   114→      headers: {
   115→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   116→        ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   117→      },
   118→      body: JSON.stringify({
   119→        message,
   120→        session_id: agentSessionId,
   121→        conversation_id: conversationId,
   122→        langfuse_session_id: langfuseSessionId,
   123→        conversation_history: conversationHistory ?? [],
   124→        ...(skills?.length ? { skills } : {}),
   125→      }),
   126→      signal,
   127→    });
   128→
   129→    if (!response.ok) {
   130→      const errorText = await response.text();
   131→      callbacks.onError(`Server error: ${errorText}`);
   132→      return;
   133→    }
   134→
   135→    await streamSSE(response, callbacks, signal);
   136→  } catch (e: unknown) {
   137→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
   138→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
   139→    callbacks.onError(msg);
   140→  }
   141→}
   142→
   143→export async function runAgentEditLoop(
   144→  newMessage: string,
   145→  conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[],
   146→  langfuseSessionId: string | null,
   147→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   148→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   149→  userSessionId?: string,
   150→  conversationId?: string | null,
   151→): Promise<void> {
   152→  try {
   153→    const response = await fetch('/api/chat/edit', {
   154→      method: 'POST',
   155→      headers: {
   156→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   157→        ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   158→      },
   159→      body: JSON.stringify({
   160→        new_message: newMessage,
   161→        conversation_history: conversationHistory,
   162→        langfuse_session_id: langfuseSessionId,
   163→        conversation_id: conversationId,
   164→      }),
   165→      signal,
   166→    });
   167→
   168→    if (!response.ok) {
   169→      const errorText = await response.text();
   170→      callbacks.onError(`Server error: ${errorText}`);
   171→      return;
   172→    }
   173→
   174→    await streamSSE(response, callbacks, signal);
   175→  } catch (e: unknown) {
   176→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
   177→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
   178→    callbacks.onError(msg);
   179→  }
   180→}
   181→
   182→function handleSSEEvent(
   183→  eventType: string,
   184→  data: Record<string, unknown>,
   185→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   186→) {
   187→  switch (eventType) {
   188→    case 'thinking':
   189→    case 'answer':
   190→      callbacks.onTextChunk(data.text as string);
   191→      break;
   192→    case 'thinking_done':
   193→      callbacks.onThinkingDone();
   194→      break;
   195→    case 'tool_call':
   196→      callbacks.onToolCall({
   197→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
   198→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
   199→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
   200→        command: (data.command as string) ?? undefined,
   201→        toolInput: (data.input as Record<string, unknown>) ?? undefined,
   202→        columns: [],
   203→        rows: [],
   204→        rowCount: 0,
   205→      });
   206→      break;
   207→    case 'tool_result': {
   208→      const result: ToolCallResult = {
   209→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
   210→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
   211→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
   212→        columns: (data.columns as string[]) ?? [],
   213→        rows: (data.rows as Record<string, unknown>[]) ?? [],
   214→        rowCount: (data.rowCount as number) ?? 0,
   215→        error: (data.error as string) ?? undefined,
   216→        output: (data.output as string) ?? undefined,
   217→        rawContent: (data.content as string) ?? undefined,
   218→        chart_spec: (data.chart_spec as { data: unknown[]; layout?: Record<string, unknown> }) ?? undefined,
   219→      };
   220→      callbacks.onToolResult(result);
   221→      break;
   222→    }
   223→    case 'subagent_start':
   224→      callbacks.onSubagentStart?.({
   225→        id: data.id as string,
   226→        name: data.name as string,
   227→        prompt: (data.prompt as string) ?? '',
   228→      });
   229→      break;
   230→    case 'subagent_end': {
   231→      type ChartSpec = { data: unknown[]; layout?: Record<string, unknown>; frames?: unknown[] };
   232→      // Backend sends chart_specs (array) for multi-chart support.
   233→      // Fall back to legacy chart_spec (single object) for backwards compat.
   234→      const rawSpecs = data.chart_specs as ChartSpec[] | undefined;
   235→      const legacySpec = data.chart_spec as ChartSpec | undefined;
   236→      const chartSpecs: ChartSpec[] = rawSpecs ?? (legacySpec ? [legacySpec] : []);
   237→
   238→      if (chartSpecs.length > 0) {
   239→        // Emit one callback per chart so each gets its own segment
   240→        for (const spec of chartSpecs) {
   241→          callbacks.onSubagentEnd?.({
   242→            id: data.id as string,
   243→            name: data.name as string,
   244→            chart_spec: spec,
   245→          });
   246→        }
   247→        // Emit text result separately (if any) so it doesn't get lost
   248→        if (data.result) {
   249→          callbacks.onSubagentEnd?.({
   250→            id: data.id as string,
   251→            name: data.name as string,
   252→            result: data.result as string,
   253→          });
   254→        }
   255→      } else {
   256→        callbacks.onSubagentEnd?.({
   257→          id: data.id as string,
   258→          name: data.name as string,
   259→          result: (data.result as string) ?? undefined,
   260→          sql_results: (data.sql_results as Array<{ sql: string; columns?: string[]; rows?: Record<string, unknown>[]; rowCount?: number; error?: string }>) ?? undefined,
   261→        });
   262→      }
   263→      break;
   264→    }
   265→    case 'user_question': {
   266→      // Normalize options: the agent may send plain strings or {label, description} objects
   267→      const rawOptions = (data.options as unknown[]) ?? [];
   268→      const normalizedOptions: UserQuestionOption[] = (Array.isArray(rawOptions) ? rawOptions : []).map(
   269→        (opt: unknown) => {
   270→          if (typeof opt === 'string') return { label: opt };
   271→          if (typeof opt === 'object' && opt !== null && 'label' in opt) return opt as UserQuestionOption;
   272→          return { label: String(opt) };
   273→        }
   274→      );
   275→      callbacks.onUserQuestion?.({
   276→        questionId: data.question_id as string,
   277→        question: data.question as string,
   278→        options: normalizedOptions,
   279→        multiSelect: (data.multi_select as boolean) ?? false,
   280→      });
   281→      break;
   282→    }
   283→    case 'done':
   284→      callbacks.onDone((data.session_id as string) ?? null);
   285→      break;
   286→    case 'error':
   287→      callbacks.onError((data.message as string) ?? 'Unknown error');
   288→      break;
   289→  }
   290→}
   291→

     1→from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Header
     2→from fastapi.responses import JSONResponse, StreamingResponse
     3→from pydantic import BaseModel
     4→
     5→from app.agent import stream_chat
     6→from app.database import Database
     7→from app.dependencies import get_session_db
     8→from app.pending_questions import pending_question_store
     9→
    10→router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["chat"])
    11→
    12→
    13→class ChatRequest(BaseModel):
    14→    message: str
    15→    session_id: str | None = None
    16→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None
    17→    conversation_history: list[dict] = []
    18→    skills: list[str] | None = None
    19→    conversation_id: str | None = None
    20→
    21→
    22→class ChatEditRequest(BaseModel):
    23→    new_message: str
    24→    conversation_history: list[dict] = []
    25→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None
    26→    conversation_id: str | None = None
    27→
    28→
    29→class QuestionResponseRequest(BaseModel):
    30→    question_id: str
    31→    answers: list[str] = []
    32→    free_text: str | None = None
    33→
    34→
    35→@router.post("/chat")
    36→async def chat(
    37→    request: ChatRequest,
    38→    db: Database = Depends(get_session_db),
    39→    x_session_id: str = Header(...),
    40→):
    41→    return StreamingResponse(
    42→        stream_chat(
    43→            request.message,
    44→            request.session_id,
    45→            db,
    46→            conversation_history=request.conversation_history or None,
    47→            langfuse_session_id=request.langfuse_session_id,
    48→            backend_session_id=x_session_id,
    49→            skills=request.skills,
    50→            conversation_id=request.conversation_id,
    51→        ),
    52→        media_type="text/event-stream",
    53→        headers={
    54→            "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
    55→            "Connection": "keep-alive",
    56→            "X-Accel-Buffering": "no",
    57→        },
    58→    )
    59→
    60→
    61→@router.post("/chat/edit")
    62→async def chat_edit(
    63→    request: ChatEditRequest,
    64→    db: Database = Depends(get_session_db),
    65→    x_session_id: str = Header(...),
    66→):
    67→    """Edit a message: start a fresh session with conversation history as context."""
    68→    return StreamingResponse(
    69→        stream_chat(
    70→            request.new_message,
    71→            session_id=None,
    72→            db=db,
    73→            conversation_history=request.conversation_history,
    74→            langfuse_session_id=request.langfuse_session_id,
    75→            backend_session_id=x_session_id,
    76→            conversation_id=request.conversation_id,
    77→        ),
    78→        media_type="text/event-stream",
    79→        headers={
    80→            "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
    81→            "Connection": "keep-alive",
    82→            "X-Accel-Buffering": "no",
    83→        },
    84→    )
    85→
    86→
    87→@router.post("/chat/respond")
    88→async def respond_to_question(
    89→    request: QuestionResponseRequest,
    90→    x_session_id: str = Header(...),
    91→):
    92→    """Respond to a pending user question from the agent."""
    93→    pending = pending_question_store.get_pending(x_session_id)
    94→    if pending is None or pending["question_id"] != request.question_id:
    95→        return JSONResponse(status_code=404, content={"error": "No pending question found"})
    96→
    97→    answer = {"answers": request.answers}
    98→    if request.free_text:
    99→        answer["free_text"] = request.free_text
   100→    pending_question_store.respond(x_session_id, request.question_id, answer)
   101→    return {"status": "ok"}
   102→

## Design: Chart Library Switch (Plotly / Vega-Lite)

### Section 1: UI — Global Toggle in Agent Panel Header

A segmented button (pill toggle) placed **to the left of the Export button** in `AgentPanel` header. Two options: **Plotly** (default) | **Vega-Lite**.

- Styled consistently with existing header buttons (12px font, 28px height, same border/color vars)
- Preference stored in `localStorage` key `chartLibrary` (`"plotly"` | `"vegalite"`)
- State managed via a new React context `ChartLibraryContext` (so both AgentPanel header and deeply nested components can read it)
- Switching only affects **new charts** going forward, not existing ones in the conversation

### Section 2: Frontend — Chart Spec Tagging & Dual Renderers

**chart_spec gets a `library` field:**
- TypeScript types updated: `chart_spec` gains `library?: 'plotly' | 'vegalite'`
- When the backend sends a `chart_spec`, it includes `library` to indicate which format the spec is in

**Dual rendering in ChartWidget area:**
- Existing `ChartWidget.tsx` stays as-is (Plotly renderer)
- New `VegaLiteChartWidget.tsx` component using `react-vega` (or `vega-embed`) to render Vega-Lite JSON specs
- `InlineQueryResult.tsx` checks `chart_spec.library` and dispatches to the correct widget
- New npm dependency: `vega`, `vega-lite`, `vega-embed` (or `react-vega` which wraps these)

**Theme support for Vega-Lite:**
- Dark/light mode handled via Vega-Lite config object (similar to Plotly's themeLayout)

### Section 3: Backend — Passing Library Preference to Agent

**API changes:**
- `ChatRequest` model gets `chart_library: str = "plotly"` field
- Frontend sends `chart_library` in the POST body of `/api/chat` (and `/api/chat/edit`)
- `stream_chat()` receives `chart_library` parameter

**System prompt modification:**
- `build_system_prompt(db, chart_library)` — the charting workflow section dynamically switches between Plotly instructions and Vega-Lite instructions based on the library parameter
- For Plotly: current instructions (Plotly traces, layout with title)
- For Vega-Lite: instructions to emit `{"$schema": "...", "mark": "...", "encoding": {...}, "data": {"values": [...]}}` format with required `title` in the `title` property

**MCP tool schema update:**
- `render_chart` tool keeps `data` and `layout` parameters (for Plotly)
- Adds `spec` parameter (object, for Vega-Lite — the full Vega-Lite spec)
- Adds `library` parameter (`"plotly"` or `"vegalite"`)
- Tool description updated to explain both modes
- Validation: for Plotly, requires `data` + `layout`; for Vega-Lite, requires `spec`

**chart_spec tagging:**
- Backend attaches `library` field to `chart_spec` in SSE events based on what the agent passed to `render_chart`

### Section 4: Data Flow Summary & Error Handling

**End-to-end flow:**
1. User toggles "Vega-Lite" in header → saved to localStorage, context updates
2. User sends a message → frontend includes `chart_library: "vegalite"` in POST body
3. Backend builds system prompt with Vega-Lite charting instructions
4. Agent calls `render_chart(library="vegalite", spec={...})` 
5. Backend validates, attaches `library: "vegalite"` to `chart_spec` in SSE
6. Frontend receives `tool_result` with `chart_spec.library === "vegalite"`
7. `InlineQueryResult` renders `VegaLiteChartWidget` instead of `ChartWidget`

**Error handling:**
- If agent emits wrong format (Plotly spec when Vega-Lite expected or vice versa), the renderer shows a graceful error message ("Chart rendering failed") rather than crashing
- `VegaLiteChartWidget` wraps vega-embed in a try/catch with fallback UI

**Testing:**
- Unit tests not needed for this feature (LLM-dependent spec generation)
- Manual testing: toggle switch, send chart request, verify correct renderer

  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.worktrees/chart-library-switch/docs/plans/2026-03-02-chart-library-switch-design.md",
  "content": "# Chart Library Switch Design\n\n## Overview\n\nAdd a global toggle in the agent panel header to switch between Plotly and Vega-Lite chart rendering. The agent emits native specs for whichever library is selected. Default is Plotly. Preference persists in localStorage.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- **Approach**: Single `render_chart` tool with a `library` parameter (Approach B)\n- **Scope**: Global toggle in header, not per-chart\n- **Re-render**: Switching only affects new charts; existing charts stay as rendered\n- **Spec generation**: Agent emits native Vega-Lite specs (not Plotly-to-Vega conversion)\n- **Persistence**: localStorage key `chartLibrary`\n\n## Section 1: UI — Global Toggle\n\nA segmented pill toggle in the `AgentPanel` header, placed to the left of the Export button.\n\n- Two options: **Plotly** (default) | **Vega-Lite**\n- Styled consistently with header buttons (12px font, 28px height, same border/color CSS vars)\n- State managed via `ChartLibraryContext` React context\n- Persisted in `localStorage` key `chartLibrary` (`\"plotly\"` | `\"vegalite\"`)\n- The `agent-panel__actions` grid expands from 2 columns to 3\n\n## Section 2: Frontend — Dual Renderers\n\n### Type changes\n\n`chart_spec` gains a `library` field in `types.ts`:\n\n```typescript\nchart_spec?: {\n  library?: 'plotly' | 'vegalite';\n  data: unknown[];\n  layout?: Record<string, unknown>;\n  frames?: unknown[];\n  spec?: Record<string, unknown>;  // Vega-Lite spec\n};\n```\n\n### New component: VegaLiteChartWidget\n\n- New file `frontend/src/components/VegaLiteChartWidget.tsx`\n- Uses `vega-embed` to render Vega-Lite JSON specs\n- Dark/light theme via Vega-Lite config object\n- Error boundary: wraps vega-embed in try/catch with fallback UI\n\n### Routing in InlineQueryResult\n\n`InlineQueryResult.tsx` checks `chart_spec.library`:\n- `\"vegalite\"` → renders `VegaLiteChartWidget`\n- `\"plotly\"` or absent → renders `ChartWidget` (existing)\n\n### New dependencies\n\n- `vega`, `vega-lite`, `vega-embed`\n\n## Section 3: Backend — Library-Aware Agent\n\n### API changes\n\n`ChatRequest` model gains:\n```python\nchart_library: str = \"plotly\"\n```\n\nFrontend sends `chart_library` in the POST body of `/api/chat` and `/api/chat/edit`.\n\n### System prompt\n\n`build_system_prompt(db, chart_library)` dynamically switches charting instructions:\n\n- **Plotly mode**: Current instructions (Plotly traces in `data`, layout with `title`)\n- **Vega-Lite mode**: Instructions to emit `render_chart(library=\"vegalite\", spec={\"$schema\": \"...\", \"mark\": \"...\", \"encoding\": {...}, \"data\": {\"values\": [...]}, \"title\": \"...\"})` format\n\n### MCP tool schema update\n\n`render_chart` tool gains:\n- `library` parameter: `\"plotly\"` (default) or `\"vegalite\"`\n- `spec` parameter: object for Vega-Lite spec (required when library is vegalite)\n- Validation: Plotly requires `data` + `layout`; Vega-Lite requires `spec`\n\n### SSE event tagging\n\nBackend attaches `library` field to `chart_spec` in SSE `tool_result` events based on what the agent passed.\n\n## Section 4: Data Flow\n\n```\n1. User toggles \"Vega-Lite\" in header → saved to localStorage, context updates\n2. User sends message → frontend includes chart_library: \"vegalite\" in POST\n3. Backend builds system prompt with Vega-Lite charting instructions\n4. Agent calls render_chart(library=\"vegalite\", spec={...})\n5. Backend validates, attaches library: \"vegalite\" to chart_spec in SSE\n6. Frontend receives tool_result with chart_spec.library === \"vegalite\"\n7. InlineQueryResult renders VegaLiteChartWidget\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n- If agent emits wrong format for the selected library, renderer shows graceful error (\"Chart rendering failed\") instead of crashing\n- `VegaLiteChartWidget` wraps vega-embed in try/catch with fallback UI\n- Missing `library` field on `chart_spec` defaults to Plotly (backward compat)\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `frontend/src/components/AgentPanel.tsx` | Add toggle UI |\n| `frontend/src/components/AgentPanel.css` | Expand actions grid, toggle styles |\n| `frontend/src/contexts/ChartLibraryContext.tsx` | New context (localStorage + state) |\n| `frontend/src/components/VegaLiteChartWidget.tsx` | New Vega-Lite renderer |\n| `frontend/src/components/InlineQueryResult.tsx` | Route to correct renderer |\n| `frontend/src/components/MessageBubble.tsx` | Pass library to chart segments |\n| `frontend/src/types.ts` | Add `library` and `spec` to chart_spec |\n| `frontend/src/agent/agentService.ts` | Send `chart_library` in POST, parse `library` from SSE |\n| `frontend/src/hooks/useAgent.ts` | Read chart library from context, pass to agentService |\n| `frontend/package.json` | Add vega, vega-lite, vega-embed |\n| `backend/app/routes/chat.py` | Add `chart_library` to request models |\n| `backend/app/agent.py` | Dynamic system prompt, pass chart_library to stream_chat |\n| `backend/app/mcp_sse.py` | Update render_chart tool schema, validation, SSE tagging |\n"
}
